{"parentUuid":"cc159621-8cde-4609-beb5-3660d5bc1cc8","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"9100aa34-3880-4efe-933a-856c1a2fa5c8","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01ED7A81VGgKn6HdUafp4BU3","type":"tool_result","content":"T07: Pages, Assigned editor and Navigation\n## Parent\n\nPart of #1\n\n## What to build\n\nThe Admin creates Pages such as About, History and Community Service, assigns each to one Editor, and controls which appear in public Navigation and in what order. Published Pages appear on the static site.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] The Admin creates a Page with a title, Slug and Markdown body, Draft or Published, an optional Assigned editor, `show_in_nav` and `nav_order`. A Page has no author and no date on the public site.\n- [ ] Only the Assigned editor and the Admin can edit a Page. Another Editor requesting it by direct URL is refused.\n- [ ] Editors cannot create or delete Pages or change assignment, Navigation or the Home flag. The Assigned editor can mark their Page Published.\n- [ ] Exactly one Page is the Home page. Flagging another moves the flag.\n- [ ] Page Slugs are unique among Pages and are locked after first Published, with the Admin able to unlock.\n- [ ] Preview works for Pages the same way as for Posts.\n- [ ] When an Assigned editor is Deactivated, their Pages become unassigned and Admin-only, and the Admin's user list shows how many Pages need a new editor.\n- [ ] `cms publish` renders Published Pages. Navigation on every public page is Home, then Pages marked for Navigation in order, then News. A Published Page not in Navigation is reachable by URL but not linked. A Draft Page is absent.\n- [ ] Home shows the Home Page followed by the 5 latest Posts.\n- [ ] Page output uses relative paths only and sanitized HTML.\n- [ ] The seed script adds Pages including a Draft and a Published one.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- T03 (#4)\n- T05 (#6)\n\n======\n## Problem Statement\n\nThe Chi Chapter of Delta Tau Delta at Kenyon College has an outdated website run by one alum who never updates it. Alumni cannot find updates, information is spread out, and active Officers cannot change anything. Prospective members, alumni, parents and Kenyon staff want to learn about the chapter's members, news, community service and history.\n\nThe chapter president needs a site that the Officers themselves can keep current, with these things never happening:\n\n- A graduated Officer can still log in and post.\n- An Editor deletes or changes the chapter history Page, or another Officer's Post.\n- A half-written Draft goes public, such as a rush announcement with the wrong date.\n- Members' personal information ends up public.\n\nThe people running it are college students who are comfortable with computers but not technical, and their roles change hands about once a year.\n\n## Solution\n\nA small CMS in which the president (Admin) and the other Officers (Editors) write Posts and Pages in a local admin console. The Admin runs `cms publish` to render the Published content into a static site, then `cms deploy` to push it to GitHub Pages after confirming. Nothing is Live until the Admin has reviewed and deployed it.\n\nThe console is deliberately plain. It has a dashboard that leads with what is waiting for release, a content list with a large status badge and only the filters that matter, and an editor with a prominent Preview. An Editor can only change their own Posts and the Pages the Admin has assigned to them. Officers who graduate are Deactivated immediately, and their past work and Byline stay.\n\nThe admin console never goes on the public internet (ADR-001). Editors work on a machine that runs the console, such as a shared chapter-house laptop.\n\n### The 7 required capabilities, for this client\n\n1. **Login with hashed passwords.** An Officer logs in with their email and password. Passwords are stored only as argon2 hashes. A wrong password gets a generic refusal that does not reveal whether the email exists. The Admin sets a temporary password for another User, who must change it at next login. The Admin's own recovery is a command on the machine.\n2. **Two roles, Admin and Editor.** The president is the Admin. Every other Officer is an Editor who writes their own Posts and edits only the Pages the Admin assigned to them. Access is enforced in code on every request, not by hiding links.\n3. **Posts.** Title, Slug, Markdown body, Draft or Published, author, and created, updated and published timestamps. Each Post shows a Byline with the author's full name, title and Term, fixed when the Post is first marked Published.\n4. **Pages in public Navigation.** Standing content such as About, History and Community Service has no author and no date. The Admin creates Pages, assigns one Editor to each, and chooses which appear in Navigation and in what order. One Page is the Home page.\n5. **Admin console.** A dashboard, a filterable content list, and an editor with Markdown Preview.\n6. **Admin-only user management with Deactivation.** The Admin creates Users, sets a role, title and Term, resets passwords, and Deactivates Users when they leave office. Deactivated Users are never deleted and cannot log in, even with an old session.\n7. **Static public site via `cms publish`.** `cms publish` renders only Published content into the static site, with sanitized Markdown and relative paths only. It shows what is new, changed or removed, and refuses if any Draft would appear. `cms deploy` is a separate command that confirms with the Admin before pushing to GitHub Pages.\n\n## User Stories\n\n### Login and accounts\n\n1. As an Officer, I want to log in with my email and password, so that I can reach the console without a separate username.\n2. As an Officer, I want to log out, so that the next person on a shared chapter laptop cannot use my account.\n3. As an Officer who typed a wrong password, I want a clear refusal that does not say whether my email exists, so that nobody can use the login form to find out who has an account.\n4. As an Officer who was just given a temporary password, I want to be made to choose my own at first login, so that the Admin never knows my real password.\n5. As an Officer choosing a password, I want to be told the minimum length, so that I do not guess at hidden rules.\n6. As the Admin, I want a session to end after 8 hours, so that a logged-in shared laptop does not stay open overnight.\n7. As the Admin, I want passwords stored only as argon2 hashes and never logged, so that a copied database does not expose anyone's password.\n8. As the Admin who forgot my own password, I want a command on the machine to reset it, so that I am not locked out when no email server exists.\n9. As the Admin setting up the CMS for the first time, I want `cms create-admin` to ask for my email and a hidden password, so that there is no default password in the code or the demo data.\n10. As an Admin, I want an anonymous visitor to be redirected to login on every admin URL, so that no admin content is ever shown to someone who is not logged in.\n\n### Roles and permissions\n\n11. As an Editor, I want to create Posts and edit only my own, so that I can do my job without being able to damage another Officer's work.\n12. As an Editor, I want to see every Post and Page in the content list but have other people's rows read-only, so that I know what is already written without being able to change it.\n13. As an Editor, I want to edit a Page the Admin assigned to me, so that I can keep my area (for example Community Service or History) current without needing the president.\n14. As an Editor, I want other Editors to be unable to edit my Assigned Page, so that my work is not overwritten by someone else.\n15. As an Editor, I want to mark my own Posts and my Assigned Pages Published, so that I am not waiting on the president for every step.\n16. As an Editor, I want to set my own Post back to Draft, so that I can take something down that I got wrong.\n17. As an Editor, I want to delete my own Drafts, so that I can clean up abandoned writing.\n18. As an Editor who tries to reach a page I am not allowed to use, I want a short message naming the current Admin and how to reach them, so that I know who to ask for higher permission.\n19. As an Editor, I want controls I cannot use to be hidden, so that the console is not cluttered with buttons that do nothing.\n20. As the Admin, I want an Editor who requests an admin-only URL directly to be refused (403 or redirect), so that hiding links is never the only protection.\n21. As the Admin, I want to edit and delete any Post or Page, so that I can correct problems when an Officer is unavailable.\n22. As the Admin, I want a deleted-Post rule that lets Editors delete only Drafts, so that a published announcement cannot be erased in one click.\n\n### Posts\n\n23. As an Editor, I want to write a Post with a title and a Markdown body, so that I can post news without knowing HTML.\n24. As an Editor, I want the Slug to be filled in from the title and editable until the Post is first Published, so that URLs are readable and I can fix awkward ones.\n25. As an Editor, I want a clear error if my Slug is already used by another Post, so that two Posts never share a URL.\n26. As a reader following a shared link, I want a Post's URL to stay the same after it is Published, so that links from alumni emails and social media do not break.\n27. As the Admin, I want to unlock a Slug after first publish when a correction is needed, so that the lock protects links but is not permanent.\n28. As an Editor, I want the Post to record who wrote it and when it was created, updated and published, so that readers and Officers can see its history.\n29. As a reader, I want each Post to show its author's full name, title and Term, so that I know who at the chapter is speaking and in which year.\n30. As an Editor, I want the Byline to be fixed when the Post is first Published, so that old Posts do not change when someone's title or Term later changes.\n31. As an Editor, I want to edit a Published Post in place and have the change reach the site at the next release, so that I can fix a typo without taking the Post down first.\n32. As an Editor, I want Posts that changed since the last Deploy to be marked in the content list, so that I can see which edits are not yet Live.\n\n### Pages and Navigation\n\n33. As the Admin, I want to create a Page and assign it to one Editor, so that every standing area of the site has a named maintainer.\n34. As the Admin, I want to choose which Pages appear in Navigation and their order, so that visitors see About, History and Community Service in a sensible order.\n35. As a visitor, I want the top of every public page to show Home, the chapter's Pages, and News, so that I can find things from anywhere.\n36. As a visitor, I want a Published Page without a Navigation entry to still be reachable by its URL, so that unlisted pages such as a rush information page can be shared directly.\n37. As the Admin, I want one Page flagged as the Home page, so that the site's front door is under my control.\n38. As the Admin, I want only the Admin to be able to delete a Page, so that an Editor cannot remove the chapter history Page.\n39. As the Admin, I want Pages to have no author and no date on the public site, so that standing content reads as the chapter's own voice.\n40. As the Admin, I want Pages whose Assigned editor was Deactivated to be left unassigned and Admin-only, so that nobody inherits access by accident.\n\n### Admin console\n\n41. As an Officer, I want a dashboard that leads with Pending release, so that I see first what is waiting to go public.\n42. As an Officer, I want the dashboard to show Drafts waiting and my own recent items, so that I can pick up where I left off.\n43. As the Admin, I want the dashboard to show Pages needing a new Assigned editor, so that turnover problems are visible.\n44. As an Officer, I want a content list with a large, clearly coloured status badge for Draft and Published, so that I can tell the status at a glance.\n45. As an Officer, I want to filter the content list by status, by type (Post or Page) and by author, so that I can find things quickly.\n46. As an Officer, I want a \"Mine\" shortcut on the content list, so that I can see only my own work in one click.\n47. As an Officer, I want the content list to have no categories, tags or bulk actions, so that it makes sense without a technical background.\n48. As an Editor, I want a Preview button right next to Save, so that I do not have to hunt for it.\n49. As an Editor, I want Preview to render the exact sanitized HTML that the Publish run would write, with the real Byline and Navigation, so that what I check is what visitors will see.\n50. As an Editor, I want Preview to say clearly that it is a draft preview and not public, so that I never confuse it with the live site.\n51. As an Editor, I want to see Markdown I typed with a script or event handler stripped in Preview, so that I find out about unsafe content before it is published.\n\n### User management\n\n52. As the Admin, I want to create a User with an email, display name, title, Term and role, so that a new Officer can start work.\n53. As the Admin, I want to change a User's role, title and Term, so that the console follows who currently holds each position.\n54. As the Admin, I want to Deactivate a User when they leave office, so that a graduated Officer can no longer log in or post.\n55. As the Admin, I want Deactivation to take effect immediately, including for sessions already open, so that there is no window in which a graduated Officer can post.\n56. As the Admin, I want Deactivated Users' Posts and Bylines to remain, so that chapter history is not rewritten when people graduate.\n57. As the Admin, I want to be unable to Deactivate or demote the last active Admin, so that the chapter is never locked out of its own site.\n58. As the Admin handing over, I want to promote my successor before Deactivating myself, so that the handover cannot leave the site without an Admin.\n59. As the Admin, I want the user list to warn me when Pages lose their Assigned editor, so that I reassign them during turnover.\n60. As the Admin, I want to reset another User's password to a temporary one, so that a forgetful Officer can recover without an email server.\n61. As an Editor, I want user management hidden and refused when I try its URLs directly, so that I cannot see or change anyone's account.\n62. As the Admin, I want a setting for the contact line shown to blocked Editors, so that the name and email they are told to contact stay current across turnover.\n\n### Markdown and safety\n\n63. As an Editor, I want basic formatting (headings, bold, italics, lists, links, quotes) to work, so that Posts are readable.\n64. As the Admin, I want all raw HTML in Markdown stripped, so that nobody can embed scripts or markup that is not part of the chapter's design.\n65. As a visitor, I want links that lead off the site to open safely, so that outside pages cannot reach back into the chapter's site.\n66. As a visitor, I want every internal link and file on the public site to use relative paths, so that the site works from the GitHub Pages project URL.\n67. As the Admin, I want all state-changing forms to carry a CSRF token and refuse requests without it, so that another website cannot make my browser change content.\n68. As the Admin, I want Users' emails and login names kept out of the public site, so that members' personal information is never published by the CMS.\n\n### Publishing and Deploy\n\n69. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to render only Published Posts and Pages into the static site, so that drafts never leave the database.\n70. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to refuse and explain when any Draft would appear in the output, so that a half-written rush announcement cannot slip through.\n71. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to show what is new, changed and removed, so that I can review a release before it goes Live.\n72. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to render Navigation, Home, News and each Post's Byline, so that the generated site is complete and I only run one command.\n73. As the Admin, I want the News page to list Posts newest-first by published date, so that alumni see the latest chapter news first.\n74. As the Admin, I want Home to show the Home Page followed by the 5 latest Posts, so that visitors see current activity straight away.\n75. As the Admin, I want `cms deploy` to ask me to confirm before it pushes, so that I give final approval to everything that becomes Live.\n76. As the Admin, I want `cms deploy` to push only the rendered static site, so that the database, `.env` and secrets are never published.\n77. As the Admin, I want unpublished (set back to Draft) content to disappear from the site at the next release, so that taking something down is possible.\n78. As a visitor (prospective member, alumni, parent or Kenyon staff), I want to read the chapter's news, community service and history without logging in, so that I can learn about the chapter.\n\n### Demo and handover\n\n79. As a grader or a new Admin, I want the demo seed script to build a demo database with an Admin, an Editor and a mix of Draft and Published content, so that I can use the CMS from a fresh clone with no manual steps.\n80. As the outgoing president, I want a handover checklist in the README, so that my successor knows what to take over (the repository, the database and the image folder) and what to do first.\n\n### Cuttable extras (built last)\n\n81. As the Admin, I want Markdown images from an admin-managed folder to appear in Posts and Pages by relative path, so that the chapter's photos can illustrate its history and service.\n82. As the Admin, I want only images referenced by Published content to be copied into the static site, so that an unapproved photo never goes public.\n83. As an Editor, I want an optional event date on a Post, so that rush and philanthropy events show when they happen.\n84. As a visitor, I want a Post with an event date to show both its published date and \"Event: <date>\", so that I can tell when something was posted from when it happens.\n85. As the Admin, I want `cms backup` to write a dated copy of the database outside the repository, so that one dead laptop does not erase the chapter's content.\n86. As the Admin, I want a username lock for 10 minutes after 5 failed logins, so that password guessing is slow. The lock is keyed on the email.\n87. As the Admin, I want `cms deploy` to warn me and stop if content has changed since the last `cms publish`, so that stale files in the static site are not pushed by accident.\n88. As the Admin, I want the `cms publish` summary to compare against what was last Deployed, so that \"changed\" means changed since the public site was updated.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n**Architecture (fixed by ADR-001 and ADR-002):** a FastAPI admin console with Jinja templates and SQLite runs locally. `cms publish` writes a static site, and `cms deploy` pushes it to the `gh-pages` branch. There is no frontend framework and no build step. This spec adds no dependency. Asking the user is required before adding one.\n\n**Modules**\n- **Users and authentication:** the User record (email, display name, title, Term, role, active flag, password hash, must-change-password flag), argon2 hashing, signed session cookies with an 8-hour lifetime, and a per-request check that rejects Deactivated Users, so old sessions stop working. Login is by email. The wrong-password message is the same whether or not the email exists.\n- **Authorization:** one place that decides \"may this User do this to this item\". Every admin route calls it on the server side. The rules:\n  - Admin may do everything.\n  - An Editor may create Posts, may edit only their own Posts, and may edit only the Pages assigned to them.\n  - Editors may set their own Posts and Assigned Pages to Published, and may set their own Posts back to Draft.\n  - Editors may delete only their own Draft Posts.\n  - Only the Admin may create or delete Pages, assign editors, set Navigation, unlock a Slug, manage Users, or reset passwords.\n  - Everything else read-only for Editors.\n- **CSRF protection:** every state-changing form carries a token, and requests without it are rejected.\n- **Content:** Posts and Pages. A Post has a title, a unique Slug, a Markdown body, status, author, created, updated and published timestamps, and a Byline snapshot (name, title, Term) taken at first Published. A Page has a title, a unique Slug, a Markdown body, status, an Assigned editor, `show_in_nav`, `nav_order`, and an `is_home` flag that is true for exactly one Page. Slugs are generated from the title, lowercase with hyphens, unique within their kind, and locked after first Published except by the Admin. Editing a Published item changes it in place.\n- **Markdown rendering:** a single function that converts Markdown to HTML and then sanitizes it, used by both Preview and the Publish run so they cannot disagree. Raw HTML is stripped. Basic formatting and links are allowed, and outside links get `rel=\"noopener\"`.\n- **Admin console:** dashboard (Pending release first, then Drafts waiting, then my recent items, and for the Admin only Pages needing an editor), content list (status, type and author filters, \"Mine\" shortcut, large status badge, read-only rows for other people's items), editor with Preview (opens in a new tab with a \"draft preview, not public\" banner, refreshed on click), user management, and the blocked-Editor message that names the Admin and the contact line.\n- **Settings:** an Admin-editable contact line shown only inside the console.\n- **Publishing:** reads only Published content, renders pages, Posts, Home, News and Navigation into the static site using relative paths only, then verifies that no Draft appears in the output. Home is the Home Page followed by the 5 latest Posts as title, date and first paragraph. News lists every Post newest-first, with titles and dates, on one page.\n- **CLI:** `cms publish` renders, shows new, changed and removed items, and refuses on any Draft in the output. `cms deploy` is separate, asks for confirmation, then pushes. `cms create-admin <email>` takes a hidden password prompt. `cms reset-password <email>` resets the Admin's own password on the machine. Both take an email, not a username, to match login.\n- **Demo data:** the seed script creates the Admin and Editor from `.env` values (never hard-coded), plus sample Posts and Pages including at least one Draft and one Published item.\n\n**Passwords and sessions:** minimum 12 characters and no other rules. Temporary passwords force a change at next login.\n\n**Cuttable extras (built last, each independent of the core)**\n- **Images:** an admin-managed folder, referenced by relative path in Markdown. The Publish run copies only images referenced by Published content. Without this extra, image syntax is stripped by the sanitizer.\n- **Event date:** an optional field on Posts. A Post with one shows both its published date and \"Event: <date>\". News is still sorted by published date.\n- **`cms backup`:** writes a timestamped copy of the database outside the repository. Never committed.\n- **Login lockout:** keyed on the email, 5 failures locks it for 10 minutes. The message does not reveal whether the email exists.\n- **Deploy fingerprints:** a successful `cms deploy` records a fingerprint of each item, the baseline for \"changed since last Deploy\" in the `cms publish` summary and for the stale-site warning in `cms deploy`. Without this extra, the summary lists every Published item.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\n**What makes a good test:** it exercises external behavior only, such as what a User sees or is refused, and what ends up in the generated files. It never reaches into private functions or table layouts. Each acceptance criterion in a ticket should have at least one test that clearly exercises it.\n\n**Two seams, both existing:**\n1. **The HTTP app through the test client** (`client` and the logged-in `client_as(\"admin\" | \"editor\")`). This covers login and logout, roles and permission refusals (including direct URL requests), Posts and Pages (Slug rules, the Draft and Published rules, Assigned editor, Byline snapshot), the dashboard, content list and filters, Preview, user management and Deactivation, and CSRF (replaying each POST with the token removed). Assertions are on status codes, redirects and response HTML.\n2. **Static output and the `cms` commands, against a temporary site folder and a temporary database.** `render_site` covers the content of the output: only Published content, no root-absolute paths, `<script>` and `onerror=` removed, Navigation order, Home and News, bylines with no email or login name. The `cms` command entry point covers `publish` refusing when a Draft would appear and `deploy` asking before pushing, with the actual push stubbed so nothing touches `gh-pages`.\n\n**Prior art:** The shared test fixtures already provides `client` and `client_as`, and the seeded users `admin@example.test` and `editor@example.test`. The T00 tests show the style for static output tests (publish into a temp folder, then read the files and check for relative paths). The T00 tests must keep passing.\n\n**Security checklist coverage:** each of the 10 items in the project's security checklist gets at least one test through the seams above, including the editor-cannot-reach-admin-routes and anonymous-is-redirected tests.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Image upload by Editors (stretch goal; for now the Admin adds photos to a folder).\n- Remote access for Editors. The console stays local (ADR-001), and live admin deployment is a stretch goal.\n- Comments, search, or any server-side feature on the public site.\n- Categories, tags and bulk actions in the content list.\n- Revision history and a separate draft copy of a Live item.\n- A public member roster page or any automated listing of members.\n- Email, including emailed password resets.\n- Pagination of News, and live-as-you-type Preview.\n- A \"protected page\" flag. Page permissions come from the Assigned editor.\n- Position-based Page assignment. Pages are assigned to a User.\n- Any change to the stack in the project's dependency list.\n\n## Further Notes\n\n- **Build order:** the 7 required capabilities come first. The five cuttable extras (images, event date, `cms backup`, login lockout, deploy fingerprints) are the last tickets so they can be cut if time runs out. The core must work without them.\n- **Vocabulary:** use the terms in `CONTEXT.md`: Officer, User, Admin, Editor, Deactivated, Term, Byline, Post, Page, Assigned editor, Draft, Published, Live, Publish run, Deploy, Pending release, Navigation and Slug. \"Published\" means ready for release and \"Live\" means actually on the public site as of the latest Deploy.\n- **Personal information:** the CMS controls only what it renders from User records. Whatever an Editor types in a body is the Editor's responsibility, and Preview shows exactly what will be public.\n- **Repo is public:** photos added to the image folder are public the moment they are pushed, so unapproved photos stay out of the folder.\n- **Source material:** the client brief and WordPress field notes in the repository's notes folder, and the grill-with-docs session decisions.","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"30510cc8-a304-4ea9-829a-abf529021376","timestamp":"2026-10-06T23:14:32.539Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"T07: Pages, Assigned editor and Navigation\n## Parent\n\nPart of #1\n\n## What to build\n\nThe Admin creates Pages such as About, History and Community Service, assigns each to one Editor, and controls which appear in public Navigation and in what order. As an Officer who was just given a temporary password, I want to be made to choose my own at first login, so that the Admin never knows my real password.\n5. As an Officer choosing a password, I want to be told the minimum length, so that I do not guess at hidden rules.\n6. As the Admin, I want a session to end after 8 hours, so that a logged-in shared laptop does not stay open overnight.\n7. As the Admin, I want passwords stored only as argon2 hashes and never logged, so that a copied database does not expose anyone's password.\n8. As the Admin who forgot my own password, I want a command on the machine to reset it, so that I am not locked out when no email server exists.\n9. As the Admin setting up the CMS for the first time, I want `cms create-admin` to ask for my email and a hidden password, so that there is no default password in the code or the demo data.\n10. As an Admin, I want an anonymous visitor to be redirected to login on every admin URL, so that no admin content is ever shown to someone who is not logged in.\n\n### Roles and permissions\n\n11. As an Editor, I want to create Posts and edit only my own, so that I can do my job without being able to damage another Officer's work.\n12. As an Editor, I want to see every Post and Page in the content list but have other people's rows read-only, so that I know what is already written without being able to change it.\n13. As an Editor, I want to edit a Page the Admin assigned to me, so that I can keep my area (for example Community Service or History) current without needing the president.\n14. As an Editor, I want other Editors to be unable to edit my Assigned Page, so that my work is not overwritten by someone else.\n15. As an Editor, I want to mark my own Posts and my Assigned Pages Published, so that I am not waiting on the president for every step.\n16. As an Editor, I want to set my own Post back to Draft, so that I can take something down that I got wrong.\n17. As an Editor, I want to delete my own Drafts, so that I can clean up abandoned writing.\n18. As an Editor who tries to reach a page I am not allowed to use, I want a short message naming the current Admin and how to reach them, so that I know who to ask for higher permission.\n19. As an Editor, I want controls I cannot use to be hidden, so that the console is not cluttered with buttons that do nothing.\n20. As the Admin, I want an Editor who requests an admin-only URL directly to be refused (403 or redirect), so that hiding links is never the only protection.\n21. As the Admin, I want to edit and delete any Post or Page, so that I can correct problems when an Officer is unavailable.\n22. As the Admin, I want a deleted-Post rule that lets Editors delete only Drafts, so that a published announcement cannot be erased in one click.\n\n### Posts\n\n23. As an Editor, I want to write a Post with a title and a Markdown body, so that I can post news without knowing HTML.\n24. As an Editor, I want the Slug to be filled in from the title and editable until the Post is first Published, so that URLs are readable and I can fix awkward ones.\n25. As an Editor, I want a clear error if my Slug is already used by another Post, so that two Posts never share a URL.\n26. As a reader following a shared link, I want a Post's URL to stay the same after it is Published, so that links from alumni emails and social media do not break.\n27. As the Admin, I want to unlock a Slug after first publish when a correction is needed, so that the lock protects links but is not permanent.\n28. As an Editor, I want the Post to record who wrote it and when it was created, updated and published, so that readers and Officers can see its history.\n29. As a reader, I want each Post to show its author's full name, title and Term, so that I know who at the chapter is speaking and in which year.\n30. As an Editor, I want the Byline to be fixed when the Post is first Published, so that old Posts do not change when someone's title or Term later changes.\n31. As an Editor, I want to edit a Published Post in place and have the change reach the site at the next release, so that I can fix a typo without taking the Post down first.\n32. As an Editor, I want Posts that changed since the last Deploy to be marked in the content list, so that I can see which edits are not yet Live.\n\n### Pages and Navigation\n\n33. As the Admin, I want to create a Page and assign it to one Editor, so that every standing area of the site has a named maintainer.\n34. As the Admin, I want to choose which Pages appear in Navigation and their order, so that visitors see About, History and Community Service in a sensible order.\n35. As a visitor, I want the top of every public page to show Home, the chapter's Pages, and News, so that I can find things from anywhere.\n36. As a visitor, I want a Published Page without a Navigation entry to still be reachable by its URL, so that unlisted pages such as a rush information page can be shared directly.\n37. As the Admin, I want one Page flagged as the Home page, so that the site's front door is under my control.\n38. As the Admin, I want only the Admin to be able to delete a Page, so that an Editor cannot remove the chapter history Page.\n39. As the Admin, I want Pages to have no author and no date on the public site, so that standing content reads as the chapter's own voice.\n40. As the Admin, I want Pages whose Assigned editor was Deactivated to be left unassigned and Admin-only, so that nobody inherits access by accident.\n\n### Admin console\n\n41. As an Officer, I want a dashboard that leads with Pending release, so that I see first what is waiting to go public.\n42. As an Officer, I want the dashboard to show Drafts waiting and my own recent items, so that I can pick up where I left off.\n43. As the Admin, I want the dashboard to show Pages needing a new Assigned editor, so that turnover problems are visible.\n44. As an Officer, I want a content list with a large, clearly coloured status badge for Draft and Published, so that I can tell the status at a glance.\n45. As an Officer, I want to filter the content list by status, by type (Post or Page) and by author, so that I can find things quickly.\n46. As an Officer, I want a \"Mine\" shortcut on the content list, so that I can see only my own work in one click.\n47. As an Officer, I want the content list to have no categories, tags or bulk actions, so that it makes sense without a technical background.\n48. As an Editor, I want a Preview button right next to Save, so that I do not have to hunt for it.\n49. As an Editor, I want Preview to render the exact sanitized HTML that the Publish run would write, with the real Byline and Navigation, so that what I check is what visitors will see.\n50. As an Editor, I want Preview to say clearly that it is a draft preview and not public, so that I never confuse it with the live site.\n51. As an Editor, I want to see Markdown I typed with a script or event handler stripped in Preview, so that I find out about unsafe content before it is published.\n\n### User management\n\n52. As the Admin, I want to create a User with an email, display name, title, Term and role, so that a new Officer can start work.\n53. As the Admin, I want to change a User's role, title and Term, so that the console follows who currently holds each position.\n54. As the Admin, I want to Deactivate a User when they leave office, so that a graduated Officer can no longer log in or post.\n55. As the Admin, I want Deactivation to take effect immediately, including for sessions already open, so that there is no window in which a graduated Officer can post.\n56. As the Admin, I want Deactivated Users' Posts and Bylines to remain, so that chapter history is not rewritten when people graduate.\n57. As the Admin, I want to be unable to Deactivate or demote the last active Admin, so that the chapter is never locked out of its own site.\n58. As the Admin handing over, I want to promote my successor before Deactivating myself, so that the handover cannot leave the site without an Admin.\n59. As the Admin, I want the user list to warn me when Pages lose their Assigned editor, so that I reassign them during turnover.\n60. As the Admin, I want to reset another User's password to a temporary one, so that a forgetful Officer can recover without an email server.\n61. As an Editor, I want user management hidden and refused when I try its URLs directly, so that I cannot see or change anyone's account.\n62. As the Admin, I want a setting for the contact line shown to blocked Editors, so that the name and email they are told to contact stay current across turnover.\n\n### Markdown and safety\n\n63. As an Editor, I want basic formatting (headings, bold, italics, lists, links, quotes) to work, so that Posts are readable.\n64. As the Admin, I want all raw HTML in Markdown stripped, so that nobody can embed scripts or markup that is not part of the chapter's design.\n65. As a visitor, I want links that lead off the site to open safely, so that outside pages cannot reach back into the chapter's site.\n66. As a visitor, I want every internal link and file on the public site to use relative paths, so that the site works from the GitHub Pages project URL.\n67. As the Admin, I want all state-changing forms to carry a CSRF token and refuse requests without it, so that another website cannot make my browser change content.\n68. As the Admin, I want Users' emails and login names kept out of the public site, so that members' personal information is never published by the CMS.\n\n### Publishing and Deploy\n\n69. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to render only Published Posts and Pages into the static site, so that drafts never leave the database.\n70. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to refuse and explain when any Draft would appear in the output, so that a half-written rush announcement cannot slip through.\n71. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to show what is new, changed and removed, so that I can review a release before it goes Live.\n72. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to render Navigation, Home, News and each Post's Byline, so that the generated site is complete and I only run one command.\n73. As the Admin, I want the News page to list Posts newest-first by published date, so that alumni see the latest chapter news first.\n74. As the Admin, I want Home to show the Home Page followed by the 5 latest Posts, so that visitors see current activity straight away.\n75. As the Admin, I want `cms deploy` to ask me to confirm before it pushes, so that I give final approval to everything that becomes Live.\n76. As the Admin, I want `cms deploy` to push only the rendered static site, so that the database, `.env` and secrets are never published.\n77. As the Admin, I want unpublished (set back to Draft) content to disappear from the site at the next release, so that taking something down is possible.\n78. As a visitor (prospective member, alumni, parent or Kenyon staff), I want to read the chapter's news, community service and history without logging in, so that I can learn about the chapter.\n\n### Demo and handover\n\n79. As a grader or a new Admin, I want the demo seed script to build a demo database with an Admin, an Editor and a mix of Draft and Published content, so that I can use the CMS from a fresh clone with no manual steps.\n80. As the outgoing president, I want a handover checklist in the README, so that my successor knows what to take over (the repository, the database and the image folder) and what to do first.\n\n### Cuttable extras (built last)\n\n81. As the Admin, I want Markdown images from an admin-managed folder to appear in Posts and Pages by relative path, so that the chapter's photos can illustrate its history and service.\n82. As the Admin, I want only images referenced by Published content to be copied into the static site, so that an unapproved photo never goes public.\n83. As an Editor, I want an optional event date on a Post, so that rush and philanthropy events show when they happen.\n84. As a visitor, I want a Post with an event date to show both its published date and \"Event: <date>\", so that I can tell when something was posted from when it happens.\n85. As the Admin, I want `cms backup` to write a dated copy of the database outside the repository, so that one dead laptop does not erase the chapter's content.\n86. As the Admin, I want a username lock for 10 minutes after 5 failed logins, so that password guessing is slow. The lock is keyed on the email.\n87. As the Admin, I want `cms deploy` to warn me and stop if content has changed since the last `cms publish`, so that stale files in the static site are not pushed by accident.\n88. As the Admin, I want the `cms publish` summary to compare against what was last Deployed, so that \"changed\" means changed since the public site was updated.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n**Architecture (fixed by ADR-001 and ADR-002):** a FastAPI admin console with Jinja templates and SQLite runs locally. `cms publish` writes a static site, and `cms deploy` pushes it to the `gh-pages` branch. There is no frontend framework and no build step. This spec adds no dependency. Asking the user is required before adding one.\n\n**Modules**\n- **Users and authentication:** the User record (email, display name, title, Term, role, active flag, password hash, must-change-password flag), argon2 hashing, signed session cookies with an 8-hour lifetime, and a per-request check that rejects Deactivated Users, so old sessions stop working. Login is by email. The wrong-password message is the same whether or not the email exists.\n- **Authorization:** one place that decides \"may this User do this to this item\". Every admin route calls it on the server side. The rules:\n  - Admin may do everything.\n  - An Editor may create Posts, may edit only their own Posts, and may edit only the Pages assigned to them.\n  - Editors may set their own Posts and Assigned Pages to Published, and may set their own Posts back to Draft.\n  - Editors may delete only their own Draft Posts.\n  - Only the Admin may create or delete Pages, assign editors, set Navigation, unlock a Slug, manage Users, or reset passwords.\n  - Everything else read-only for Editors.\n- **CSRF protection:** every state-changing form carries a token, and requests without it are rejected.\n- **Content:** Posts and Pages. A Post has a title, a unique Slug, a Markdown body, status, author, created, updated and published timestamps, and a Byline snapshot (name, title, Term) taken at first Published. A Page has a title, a unique Slug, a Markdown body, status, an Assigned editor, `show_in_nav`, `nav_order`, and an `is_home` flag that is true for exactly one Page. Slugs are generated from the title, lowercase with hyphens, unique within their kind, and locked after first Published except by the Admin. Editing a Published item changes it in place.\n- **Markdown rendering:** a single function that converts Markdown to HTML and then sanitizes it, used by both Preview and the Publish run so they cannot disagree. Raw HTML is stripped. Basic formatting and links are allowed, and outside links get `rel=\"noopener\"`.\n- **Admin console:** dashboard (Pending release first, then Drafts waiting, then my recent items, and for the Admin only Pages needing an editor), content list (status, type and author filters, \"Mine\" shortcut, large status badge, read-only rows for other people's items), editor with Preview (opens in a new tab with a \"draft preview, not public\" banner, refreshed on click), user management, and the blocked-Editor message that names the Admin and the contact line.\n- **Settings:** an Admin-editable contact line shown only inside the console.\n- **Publishing:** reads only Published content, renders pages, Posts, Home, News and Navigation into the static site using relative paths only, then verifies that no Draft appears in the output. Home is the Home Page followed by the 5 latest Posts as title, date and first paragraph. News lists every Post newest-first, with titles and dates, on one page.\n- **CLI:** `cms publish` renders, shows new, changed and removed items, and refuses on any Draft in the output. `cms deploy` is separate, asks for confirmation, then pushes. `cms create-admin <email>` takes a hidden password prompt. `cms reset-password <email>` resets the Admin's own password on the machine. Both take an email, not a username, to match login.\n- **Demo data:** the seed script creates the Admin and Editor from `.env` values (never hard-coded), plus sample Posts and Pages including at least one Draft and one Published item.\n\n**Passwords and sessions:** minimum 12 characters and no other rules. Temporary passwords force a change at next login.\n\n**Cuttable extras (built last, each independent of the core)**\n- **Images:** an admin-managed folder, referenced by relative path in Markdown. The Publish run copies only images referenced by Published content. Without this extra, image syntax is stripped by the sanitizer.\n- **Event date:** an optional field on Posts. A Post with one shows both its published date and \"Event: <date>\". News is still sorted by published date.\n- **`cms backup`:** writes a timestamped copy of the database outside the repository. Never committed.\n- **Login lockout:** keyed on the email, 5 failures locks it for 10 minutes. The message does not reveal whether the email exists.\n- **Deploy fingerprints:** a successful `cms deploy` records a fingerprint of each item, the baseline for \"changed since last Deploy\" in the `cms publish` summary and for the stale-site warning in `cms deploy`. Without this extra, the summary lists every Published item.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\n**What makes a good test:** it exercises external behavior only, such as what a User sees or is refused, and what ends up in the generated files. It never reaches into private functions or table layouts. Each acceptance criterion in a ticket should have at least one test that clearly exercises it.\n\n**Two seams, both existing:**\n1. **The HTTP app through the test client** (`client` and the logged-in `client_as(\"admin\" | \"editor\")`). This covers login and logout, roles and permission refusals (including direct URL requests), Posts and Pages (Slug rules, the Draft and Published rules, Assigned editor, Byline snapshot), the dashboard, content list and filters, Preview, user management and Deactivation, and CSRF (replaying each POST with the token removed). Assertions are on status codes, redirects and response HTML.\n2. **Static output and the `cms` commands, against a temporary site folder and a temporary database.** `render_site` covers the content of the output: only Published content, no root-absolute paths, `<script>` and `onerror=` removed, Navigation order, Home and News, bylines with no email or login name. The `cms` command entry point covers `publish` refusing when a Draft would appear and `deploy` asking before pushing, with the actual push stubbed so nothing touches `gh-pages`.\n\n**Prior art:** The shared test fixtures already provides `client` and `client_as`, and the seeded users `admin@example.test` and `editor@example.test`. The T00 tests show the style for static output tests (publish into a temp folder, then read the files and check for relative paths). The T00 tests must keep passing.\n\n**Security checklist coverage:** each of the 10 items in the project's security checklist gets at least one test through the seams above, including the editor-cannot-reach-admin-routes and anonymous-is-redirected tests.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Image upload by Editors (stretch goal; for now the Admin adds photos to a folder).\n- Remote access for Editors. The console stays local (ADR-001), and live admin deployment is a stretch goal.\n- Comments, search, or any server-side feature on the public site.\n- Categories, tags and bulk actions in the content list.\n- Revision history and a separate draft copy of a Live item.\n- A public member roster page or any automated listing of members.\n- Email, including emailed password resets.\n- Pagination of News, and live-as-you-type Preview.\n- A \"protected page\" flag. Page permissions come from the Assigned editor.\n- Position-based Page assignment. Pages are assigned to a User.\n- Any change to the stack in the project's dependency list.\n\n## Further Notes\n\n- **Build order:** the 7 required capabilities come first. The five cuttable extras (images, event date, `cms backup`, login lockout, deploy fingerprints) are the last tickets so they can be cut if time runs out. The core must work without them.\n- **Vocabulary:** use the terms in `CONTEXT.md`: Officer, User, Admin, Editor, Deactivated, Term, Byline, Post, Page, Assigned editor, Draft, Published, Live, Publish run, Deploy, Pending release, Navigation and Slug. \"Published\" means ready for release and \"Live\" means actually on the public site as of the latest Deploy.\n- **Personal information:** the CMS controls only what it renders from User records. Whatever an Editor types in a body is the Editor's responsibility, and Preview shows exactly what will be public.\n- **Repo is public:** photos added to the image folder are public the moment they are pushed, so unapproved photos stay out of the folder.\n- **Source material:** the client brief and WordPress field notes in the repository's notes folder, and the grill-with-docs session decisions.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"serverClassifierContext":{"request":"51124c1b-ac95-4345-8fb7-9a3703fba41b","context":{"git_state":{"cwd":"/home/faisa/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","root":"/home/faisa/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","branch":"main","default_branch":"main","status":{"clean":false,"counts":{"staged":0,"modified":1,"untracked":null,"untracked_normal":3},"porcelain":null,"truncated":false},"visibility":{"origin":{"host":"github.com","remote":"faisalsyed922-cloud/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"},"push_remote":"origin","remotes":[{"name":"origin","host":"github.com","remote":"faisalsyed922-cloud/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}],"visibility_cache":[{"host":"github.com","remote":"faisalsyed922-cloud/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}]}},"live_cwd":"/home/faisa/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","platform":"wsl"}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"cc159621-8cde-4609-beb5-3660d5bc1cc8","session_id":"4b1a37e3-ea11-48e9-baa9-b66b752b7a0d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/faisa/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"7000aa97-0f97-4842-acdc-fc51b49f56dc","version":"2.1.292","gitBranch":"main"}

starlette.middleware.sessions import SessionMiddleware\n\nfrom app import posts, settings, users\nfrom app.routes import auth, posts as posts_routes, user_password, users as users_routes\nfrom app.security import SESSION_SECONDS, AdminRequired, LoginRequired, PasswordChangeRequired, csrf_token, current_user\nfrom app.templating import templates\n\n\ndef create_app(database: Path | None = None) -> FastAPI:\n    app = FastAPI(title=\"Chi Chapter CMS\")\n    app.state.database = Path(database or settings.DATABASE_PATH)\n    users.init_db(app.state.database)\n    posts.init_posts(app.state.database)\n    app.add_middleware(SessionMiddleware, secret_key=settings.SECRET_KEY,\n                       max_age=SESSION_SECONDS, same_site=\"lax\")\n\n    @app.exception_handler(LoginRequired)\n    def send_to_login(request: Request, exc: LoginRequired):\n        return RedirectResponse(\"/login\", status_code=303)\n\n    @app.exception_handler(PasswordChangeRequired)\n    def must_change(request: Request, exc: PasswordChangeRequired):\n        return RedirectResponse(\"/user/password\", status_code=303)\n\n    @app.exception_handler(AdminRequired)\n    def admin_only(request: Request, exc: AdminRequired):\n        db = request.app.state.database\n        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n            request, \"admin/forbidden.html\",\n            {\"title\": \"Admin only\", \"admin\": users.current_admin(db),\n             \"contact_line\": users.get_setting(db, \"contact_line\")},\n            status_code=403)\n\n    app.include_router(auth.router)\n    app.include_router(user_password.router)\n    app.include_router(users_routes.router)\n    app.include_router(posts_routes.router)\n\n    @app.get(\"/admin\")\n    def admin_home(request: Request, user=Depends(current_user)):\n        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n            request, \"admin/hello.html\",\n            {\"title\": \"Admin\", \"user\": user, \"csrf_token\": csrf_token(request)}\n        )\n\n    @app.get(\"/\")\n    def public_home(request: Request):\n        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n            request, \"public/home.html\",\n            {\"title\": settings.SITE_TITLE, \"subtitle\": settings.SITE_SUBTITLE,\n             \"items\": []},\n        )\n\n    # Your ticket work plugs in here, e.g.\n    #   from app.routes import posts\n    #   app.include_router(posts.router)\n    return app\n\n\napp = create_app()\n\"\"\"One place that decides \"may this User do this to this item\".\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\n\ndef is_admin(user) -> bool:\n    return user[\"role\"] == \"admin\"\n\n\ndef can_edit_post(user, post) -> bool:\n    return is_admin(user) or post[\"author_id\"] == user[\"id\"]\n\n\ndef can_delete_post(user, post) -> bool:\n    if is_admin(user):\n        return True\n    return post[\"author_id\"] == user[\"id\"] and post[\"status\"] == \"draft\"\n\n\ndef can_edit_slug(user, post) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Admin always; an Editor only while the Slug is not locked.\"\"\"\n    return is_admin(user) or not post[\"slug_locked\"]\n\n\ndef can_unlock_slug(user) -> bool:\n    return is_admin(user)\n\"\"\"Post storage. A Post's Byline is copied from its author at first Published.\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport re\nimport sqlite3\nimport unicodedata\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom app.users import connect\n\n_SCHEMA = \"\"\"\ncreate table if not exists posts (\n    id integer primary key,\n    title text not null,\n    slug text not null unique,\n    body text not null default '',\n    status text not null default 'draft' check (status in ('draft', 'published')),\n    author_id integer not null references users(id),\n    created_at text not null,\n    updated_at text not null,\n    published_at text,\n    slug_locked integer not null default 0,\n    byline_name text,\n    byline_title text,\n    byline_term text\n)\n\"\"\"\n\n\nclass DuplicateSlug(Exception):\n    pass\n\n\ndef now() -> str:\n    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec=\"seconds\")\n\n\ndef slugify(title: str) -> str:\n    text = unicodedata.normalize(\"NFKD\", title).encode(\"ascii\", \"ignore\").decode()\n    return re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]+\", \"-\", text.lower()).strip(\"-\") or \"post\"\n\n\ndef init_posts(database: Path) -> None:\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        conn.execute(_SCHEMA)\n\n\ndef get_post(database: Path, post_id: int) -> sqlite3.Row | None:\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        return conn.execute(\n            \"select posts.*, users.display_name as author_name from posts\"\n            \" join users on users.id = posts.author_id where posts.id = ?\",\n            (post_id,)).fetchone()\n\n\ndef list_posts(database: Path) -> list[sqlite3.Row]:\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        return conn.execute(\n            \"select posts.*, users.display_name as author_name from posts\"\n            \" join users on users.id = posts.author_id\"\n            \" order by posts.updated_at desc, posts.id desc\").fetchall()\n\n\ndef create_post(database: Path, *, author, title: str, slug: str, body: str,\n                publish: bool = False) -> int:\n    stamp = now()\n    try:\n        with connect(database) as conn:\n            cur = conn.execute(\n                \"insert into posts (title, slug, body, author_id, created_at,\"\n                \" updated_at) values (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)\",\n                (title, slug or slugify(title), body, author[\"id\"], stamp, stamp))\n            post_id = cur.lastrowid\n            if publish:\n                _publish(conn, post_id, author, stamp)\n            return post_id\n    except sqlite3.IntegrityError as exc:\n        if \"posts.slug\" in str(exc):\n            raise DuplicateSlug from exc\n        raise\n\n\ndef update_post(database: Path, post_id: int, *, title: str, body: str,\n                slug: str | None, action: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Save changes. `slug` is None when the caller may not change it.\"\"\"\n    stamp = now()\n    try:\n        with connect(database) as conn:\n            if slug is None:\n                conn.execute(\"update posts set title=?, body=?, updated_at=?\"\n                             \" where id=?\", (title, body, stamp, post_id))\n            else:\n                conn.execute(\"update posts set title=?, body=?, slug=?,\"\n                             \" updated_at=? where id=?\",\n                             (title, body, slug or slugify(title), stamp, post_id))\n            if action == \"publish\":\n                author = conn.execute(\n                    \"select users.* from users join posts\"\n                    \" on posts.author_id = users.id where posts.id=?\",\n                    (post_id,)).fetchone()\n                _publish(conn, post_id, author, stamp)\n            elif action == \"draft\":\n                conn.execute(\"update posts set status='draft' where id=?\",\n                             (post_id,))\n    except sqlite3.IntegrityError as exc:\n        if \"posts.slug\" in str(exc):\n            raise DuplicateSlug from exc\n        raise\n\n\ndef _publish(conn: sqlite3.Connection, post_id: int, author, stamp: str) -> None:\n    first = conn.execute(\"select published_at from posts where id=?\",\n                         (post_id,)).fetchone()[\"published_at\"] is None\n    conn.execute(\"update posts set status='published' where id=?\", (post_id,))\n    if first:  # published timestamp, Byline and Slug lock are fixed at first publish\n        conn.execute(\n            \"update posts set published_at=?, slug_locked=1, byline_name=?,\"\n            \" byline_title=?, byline_term=? where id=?\",\n            (stamp, author[\"display_name\"], author[\"title\"], author[\"term\"],\n             post_id))\n\n\ndef unlock_slug(database: Path, post_id: int) -> None:\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        conn.execute(\"update posts set slug_locked=0 where id=?\", (post_id,))\n\n\ndef delete_post(database: Path, post_id: int) -> None:\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        conn.execute(\"delete from posts where id=?\", (post_id,))\n\n\ndef format_byline(name, title, term) -> str:\n    return \", \".join(p for p in (name, title, term) if p)\n\n\ndef byline(post) -> str:\n    return format_byline(post[\"byline_name\"], post[\"byline_title\"],\n                         post[\"byline_term\"])\n\"\"\"User accounts: storage, password hashing, and credential checks.\n\nPasswords are only ever stored as argon2 hashes. Nothing here logs a password.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport secrets\nimport sqlite3\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom argon2 import PasswordHasher\nfrom argon2.exceptions import InvalidHashError, VerifyMismatchError\n\nROLES = (\"admin\", \"editor\")\n\n_SCHEMA = \"\"\"\ncreate table if not exists users (\n    id integer primary key,\n    email text not null unique,\n    display_name text not null,\n    title text not null default '',\n    term text not null default '',\n    role text not null check (role in ('admin', 'editor')),\n    is_active integer not null default 1,\n    password_hash text not null,\n    must_change_password integer not null default 0\n)\n\"\"\"\n\n_SETTINGS_SCHEMA = \"\"\"\ncreate table if not exists settings (\n    key text primary key,\n    value text not null\n)\n\"\"\"\n\nMIN_PASSWORD = 12\n\n\ndef password_problem(password: str) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"The one place the password rule lives: a message if it fails, else None.\"\"\"\n    if len(password) < MIN_PASSWORD:\n        return f\"The password must be at least {MIN_PASSWORD} characters.\"\n    return None\n\n\nclass LastAdmin(Exception):\n    \"\"\"The change would leave the chapter with no active Admin.\"\"\"\n\n\n_hasher = PasswordHasher()\n# Verified against when the email is unknown, so both failures take similar time.\n_DUMMY_HASH = _hasher.hash(\"not-a-real-password\")\n\n\ndef connect(database: Path) -> sqlite3.Connection:\n    conn = sqlite3.connect(database)\n    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row\n    return conn\n\n\ndef init_db(database: Path) -> None:\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        conn.execute(_SCHEMA)\n        columns = {r[\"name\"] for r in conn.execute(\"pragma table_info(users)\")}\n        if \"must_change_password\" not in columns:\n            conn.execute(\"alter table users add column must_change_password\"\n                         \" integer not null default 0\")\n        conn.execute(_SETTINGS_SCHEMA)\n\n\ndef create_user(database: Path, *, email: str, password: str, role: str,\n                display_name: str, title: str = \"\", term: str = \"\") -> int:\n    if role not in ROLES:\n        raise ValueError(f\"role must be one of {ROLES}\")\n    problem = password_problem(password)\n    if problem:\n        raise ValueError(problem)\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        cur = conn.execute(\n            \"insert into users (email, display_name, title, term, role,\"\n            \" password_hash) values (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)\",\n            (email.strip().lower(), display_name, title, term, role,\n             _hasher.hash(password)),\n        )\n        return cur.lastrowid\n\n\ndef get_user(database: Path, user_id: int) -> sqlite3.Row | None:\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        return conn.execute(\"select * from users where id = ?\",\n                            (user_id,)).fetchone()\n\n\ndef authenticate(database: Path, email: str, password: str) -> sqlite3.Row | None:\n    \"\"\"Return the active user for these credentials, or None for any failure.\"\"\"\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        user = conn.execute(\"select * from users where email = ?\",\n                            (email.strip().lower(),)).fetchone()\n    stored = user[\"password_hash\"] if user else _DUMMY_HASH\n    try:\n        _hasher.verify(stored, password)\n    except (VerifyMismatchError, InvalidHashError):\n        return None\n    if user is None or not user[\"is_active\"]:\n        return None\n    return user\n\n\ndef get_user_by_email(database: Path, email: str) -> sqlite3.Row | None:\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        return conn.execute(\"select * from users where email = ?\",\n                            (email.strip().lower(),)).fetchone()\n\n\ndef set_password(database: Path, user_id: int, password: str, *,\n                 temporary: bool = False) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Store a new argon2 hash. A temporary one must be changed at next login.\"\"\"\n    problem = password_problem(password)\n    if problem:\n        raise ValueError(problem)\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        conn.execute(\"update users set password_hash = ?,\"\n                     \" must_change_password = ? where id = ?\",\n                     (_hasher.hash(password), int(temporary), user_id))\n\n\ndef verify_password(user: sqlite3.Row, password: str) -> bool:\n    try:\n        return _hasher.verify(user[\"password_hash\"], password)\n    except (VerifyMismatchError, InvalidHashError):\n        return False\n\n\ndef make_temporary_password() -> str:\n    return secrets.token_urlsafe(12)\n\n\ndef list_users(database: Path) -> list[sqlite3.Row]:\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        return conn.execute(\n            \"select * from users order by is_active desc, display_name\").fetchall()\n\n\ndef _other_active_admins(conn: sqlite3.Connection, user_id: int) -> int:\n    return conn.execute(\n        \"select count(*) from users where role = 'admin' and is_active = 1\"\n        \" and id != ?\", (user_id,)).fetchone()[0]\n\n\ndef _would_orphan_admin(conn: sqlite3.Connection, user_id: int) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"True if user_id is the only active Admin.\"\"\"\n    current = conn.execute(\"select role, is_active from users where id = ?\",\n                           (user_id,)).fetchone()\n    return bool(current and current[\"role\"] == \"admin\" and current[\"is_active\"]\n                and _other_active_admins(conn, user_id) == 0)\n\n\ndef update_user(database: Path, user_id: int, *, display_name: str, title: str,\n                term: str, role: str) -> None:\n    if role not in ROLES:\n        raise ValueError(f\"role must be one of {ROLES}\")\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        if role != \"admin\" and _would_orphan_admin(conn, user_id):\n            raise LastAdmin\n        conn.execute(\"update users set display_name = ?, title = ?, term = ?,\"\n                     \" role = ? where id = ?\",\n                     (display_name, title, term, role, user_id))\n\n\ndef deactivate_user(database: Path, user_id: int) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Deactivate, never delete: Posts and Bylines stay.\"\"\"\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        if _would_orphan_admin(conn, user_id):\n            raise LastAdmin\n        conn.execute(\"update users set is_active = 0 where id = ?\", (user_id,))\n\n\ndef current_admin(database: Path) -> sqlite3.Row | None:\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        return conn.execute(\n            \"select * from users where role = 'admin' and is_active = 1\"\n            \" order by id desc limit 1\").fetchone()\n\n\ndef get_setting(database: Path, key: str, default: str = \"\") -> str:\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        row = conn.execute(\"select value from settings where key = ?\",\n                           (key,)).fetchone()\n    return row[\"value\"] if row else default\n\n\ndef set_setting(database: Path, key: str, value: str) -> None:\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        conn.execute(\"insert into settings (key, value)
